The bf16 K = 8 cell is the known outlier of the drafting task: its `json_struct` output diverges from the minimal loop at a near-tie into a more repetitive text (2.44 bytes per round against 2.13), which is a text effect (+21% measured against +0.9% predicted); on the two prompts with identical output K = 8 is 3.343 ms/byte against 3.367 at K = 6 and 3.397 for `steps` K = 6. bf16 `fused` and `steps` produce the same text on the same prompts (3 of 5 equal to the minimal loop's at K = 6); in int4 all 5 of 5 for every cell.

**Exactness (H33).** `tools/draft_exact.py` with `configs/parallel/bolmo-fp32-fused.toml` (fused, combined drafter, graphs up to K = 32, `k_max` = 32), compared with the recorded fp32 minimal loop on atoms (byte and boundary flag): **30 of 30 identical at 256 new bytes and 30 of 30 at 1024** (`results/parallel/exact/`; the two unicode prompts that stop early in the truth are compared at their length, as in the drafting task). Kernel-level error (`tests/test_fused_kernels_gpu.py -s`, normwise: max abs error divided by the largest magnitude of the reference), fused kernels against T sequential library step calls over T = 1..33 and all L:

| inputs | head dim qk | h | committed state |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| fp32 | 64 (Bolmo) | 6.1e-7 | 2.0e-7 |
| fp32 | 128 | 5.9e-6 | 1.3e-7 |
| bf16 | 64 (Bolmo) | 2.2e-6 | 0 |
| bf16 | 128 | 0 | 0 |

(The fp32 differences are summation order and fused multiply-add, at the 1e-7 level of fp32 rounding; the 128 case sums two blocks over a wider reduction.)

### Report

1. **Hypotheses and predictions:** copied before any measurement (top of this section; the prefix of this file was diffed against `results/FINDINGS.before_parallel.md` at the end), verdicts above: H29 half and half, H30 to H32 refuted, H33 supported.
2. **Stage A0 tables:** growth per kernel class, the K = 0 -> 1 jump per class, and the int4 M scaling are above.
3. **Round cost vs K for both paths:** above (F6).
4. **End to end:** above, with the cost model's predictions next to the measurements.
5. **Exactness and numerical error:** above.
6. **Is local verification now cheap enough for multi-patch drafts with large K (input for Task B)?** In bf16, mostly yes, in relative terms: a K = 16 round costs 1.34x a K = 0 round (+0.48 ms) and K = 32 costs 1.41x (+0.59 ms), against 7.7 ms for one global pass, and the recurrence part no longer matters (4.5 us per position, all five layers); but the cost is not flat: about 10 us per position at large K, almost all of it in the batched bf16 matmuls, with a step of about 95 us when M passes 16. In int4 no: K = 16 costs 2.09x a K = 0 round (+0.85 ms against a 3.8 ms global pass) and K = 32 costs 3.05x, because tinygemm adds about 34 us per extra row; int4 multi-patch drafts with large K would need an M-friendly low-bit matmul first (a custom kernel for M up to 33), which is a different piece of work than the recurrence. For within-patch drafting, large K buys nothing in either precision (acceptance saturates at the patch length; best K stays 4-8), so the fused path gives only about 1% there; its value is the cheaper marginal row for Task B's long drafts. The cheaper levers revealed by A0 are not in the recurrence: the M = 1 -> 2 kernel switch (about 0.3 ms of every K >= 1 round in bf16; a GEMV-style kernel for M <= 8 would remove it and the M = 17 step) and the tinygemm growth in int4.

### Deviations, limits, problems

- **Premise corrected:** the state is 512 KiB per layer (head dim 64), not 1 MiB; the earlier note had the same error (erratum added to `notes/bolmo_decode_loop.md`).
- **Config limit raised:** `draft.ks` and `k_max` accepted up to 16; now up to 32 (validation only). `draft.verify` is new; the default is today's path, the default-path tests were extended and pass.
- **Attribution method:** one trace per precision and path (30 rounds per K), no repetition; the class sums reproduce the microbenchmark to within 4%, but the 63% / 57% of H29 are single-trace numbers, and a threshold of 70% against 63% is a clear miss but not by a large margin. The first synchronised-per-round trace was discarded (clock effects, above). `lm_head` is identified as the last linear kernel of the round, and "other" at K = 0 (33 kernels, 169 us) is whatever the minimal loop's graph uses for M = 1 (dot/reduce kernels): I classified by name, not by tensor.
- **Fused round costs** use whatever L the leftover drafts give (`fused`) and L = 0 (`fused (L = 0)`); the cost model uses L = 0 costs. The L = K worst case was not benchmarked end to end (see above).
- **Tuning on a microbenchmark:** block size and warps were chosen on the kernel timing at T = 1..33 for one layer; the choice changes fp32 rounding slightly relative to the library (bf16 bit-exactness of the first version was lost); H33 allows this.
- **End to end:** the cells use the K graphs {0, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8}; the larger graphs (up to 32) exist only in the benchmark and the fp32 exactness runs. The speed numbers of `steps` here (3.352 and 1.715) differ from the drafting task's (3.339 and 1.703) by 0.4-0.7%, the day-to-day drift. The bf16 `fused` K = 8 cell is dominated by a text divergence and is reported, not used.
- **Not done:** one launch for the five lazy commits (about 10 us); a GEMV-style bf16 kernel for M of 2 to 8 and a better int4 kernel for M > 2, the two things the profile points at; fusing anything else (nothing else lives on the device). Nothing was committed to git; the Colab notebook was not updated.

## Crossing drafts

Task: `TASK_crossing_drafts.md` (Task B). Builds on `## Drafting` and `## Parallel local verification` (Task A).

### Pre-registered hypotheses and predictions (copied verbatim before any measurement)

The text below, from the task file `TASK_crossing_drafts.md`, is not edited.


Copy this section **verbatim** into a new `## Crossing drafts` section of
`FINDINGS.md` **before** any measurement. Never edit it afterwards. Mark each
supported, refuted or not tested, with numbers.

Predictions for the record: flags agree with dolma2 re-tokenization on about 95%
of positions; a drafter of 135–500M parameters reaches 5–8 emitted bytes per
round; multi-patch drafting then gains 15–30% over within-patch drafting in bf16,
less in int4; with OLMo 2 using the same drafter, the byte tax falls from 2.37x
but stays above 1.5x.

- **H34 (flags):** Bolmo's emitted boundary flags agree with the dolma2
  tokenization of its own output (excluding the final, possibly incomplete
  token) on at least 95% of byte positions, and on at least 90% of patches
  exactly (same start and end).
- **H35 (break-even):** the extended cost model, with Task A's round costs,
  predicts that multi-patch drafting beats within-patch drafting only when
  emitted bytes per round exceed 1.2x the bytes per patch. (Stated now so the
  model can't be fitted to the outcome.)
- **H36 (acceptance):** at least one small token-model drafter (≤ 500M
  parameters) reaches at least 5 emitted bytes per round under the multi-patch
  rule, at K ≤ 32 bytes, on the extended prompt set at 256 new bytes.
- **H37 (predicted gain):** with the best drafter, its own cost included, the
  cost model predicts multi-patch drafting at least 15% faster than the best
  within-patch drafting in bf16.
- **H38 (byte tax, if built):** with both models using the same small drafter,
  the bf16 Bolmo/OLMo 2 ratio is below 2.0x.

### Verdicts at a glance

| Hypothesis | Verdict | Numbers |
|---|---|---|
| **H34** flags | **Supported** | Bolmo's emitted flags vs the dolma2 tokenization of prompt + output (final token excluded), bf16 ground truth, 30 prompts: **98.70%** of byte positions (256 bytes; 30,453 positions at 1024 bytes: 98.72%) and **95.0%** of patches exactly (256 bytes; 94.8% at 1024), limits 95% / 90%. Not uniform: German prose 95.7% / **80.6%** (worst prompt 91% / **51%**), Unicode mix 98.9% / 95.6%, JSON 99.2% / 97.8%, code 99.9% / 99.4%, English 99.9% / 99.7% |
| **H35** break-even | **Refuted as stated (threshold too high)** | the model with Task A's round costs and the measured B(P) gives a break-even of **1.01-1.07x** the bytes per patch in bf16 (K = 8 / 16 / 32) and 1.02-1.18x in int4, not 1.2x; a free, accuracy-limited drafter beats within-patch drafting as soon as it emits about one patch per round, because B(P) is nearly flat (7.69 -> 8.33 ms for P = 1 -> 8 in bf16) |
| **H36** acceptance | **Refuted** | best of three drafters <= 500M parameters: **4.52** emitted bytes per round (SmolLM2-360M, raw context, full-text flags, K = 32), 4.47 with healing; 4.19 for SmolLM2-135M and 4.46 for Qwen2.5-0.5B; with the task's literal `cut` protocol 4.38 (360M). Limit 5. By prompt type Qwen reaches 6.0 on Python and 5.4 on JSON, but only 3.6 on English prose, 3.9 on German |
| **H37** predicted gain | **Refuted** | with the drafter's own cost included, the best token-LM cell is **212% slower** than the best within-patch drafting in bf16 (10.57 vs 3.386 ms/byte, default prompts). Even at the weight-bandwidth floor (no launch cost) it is 33.5% slower (4.52); only a **free** drafter (not realizable) would give +22.4% (2.63) |
| **H38** byte tax | **Not tested** | gate not met (below). For the record the model predicts a ratio of 3.48 (Bolmo 10.57 vs OLMo 3.04 ms/byte) with the best LM cell for each model, against 2.35 with today's drafting |

Predictions for the record: flags agree on about 95% (got 98.7% per byte, 95.0% per patch: right); drafter reaches 5-8 bytes per round (got 4.2-4.5: **too high**); multi-patch gains 15-30% over within-patch in bf16 (predicted **-33% to -212%** with a real drafter): **wrong in sign**; byte tax falls from 2.37x but stays above 1.5x (model predicts it rises).

### Stage B0.1: flag agreement (`tools/cross_flag_agreement.py`, `results/crossing/b0/flags.{md,json}`)

Evaluated positions: a_0 .. a_n of the recorded bf16 truth (`results/drafting/truth/bf16.json`), flag on the last byte of a token/patch, tokens from the dolma2 tokenizer (`allenai/OLMo-2-0425-1B` tokenizer.json) mapped back to exact byte spans through the GPT-2 byte table (a token can end inside a UTF-8 character). The alignment was checked: shifting Bolmo's flags by one byte either way drops agreement to 54% and exact patches to 2%, so "flag on the last byte of a token" is the right convention.

| prompts (256 new bytes) | positions | byte agreement | patches | patches exact |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| English prose | 1528 | 99.93% | 314 | 99.68% |
| Python | 1524 | 99.87% | 359 | 99.44% |
| German prose | 1522 | 95.66% | 329 | 80.55% |
| JSON | 1525 | 99.15% | 498 | 97.79% |
| Unicode mix | 1526 | 98.89% | 569 | 95.61% |
| **all 30 prompts** | 7625 | **98.70%** | 2069 | **95.02%** |

Disagreement kinds at 256 bytes (99 positions; `extra` = Bolmo cuts where dolma2 does not): missing boundary inside a word 60%, missing boundary at a multi-byte character 26%, extra boundary at a multi-byte character 10%, extra inside a word 3%, one extra boundary in whitespace (Python indentation). At 1024 bytes (389 positions): the same ordering (58% / 24% / 11% / 4%), plus a few whitespace and punctuation cases. Digits caused none. The German prompts are the problem (compound words: Bolmo keeps `Eisenbahn` together where dolma2 splits it, and the umlaut cases `Fürth`, `Sändische`). The prefill's prompt mask agrees at 98.2% / 93.5% with the tokenization of the prompt alone.

### Stage B0.2-B0.3: drafters and acceptance (`tools/cross_record_drafts.py`, `cross_sim_report.py`, `cross_sim_md.py`; `results/crossing/b0/sim.md`)

Drafters (Apache-2.0, open weights, revisions pinned): SmolLM2-135M `HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM2-135M` @ `93efa2f0`, SmolLM2-360M @ `f8027fd0`, Qwen2.5-0.5B `Qwen/Qwen2.5-0.5B` @ `060db649`. Baseline: the existing combined drafter (context min match 3, n-gram count 1, order 6), flags from dolma2 re-tokenization. Peak VRAM in the recording runs 0.7-1.5 GiB (the Bolmo loop itself was not running).

Method: because a draft depends on the context only, the drafter's greedy 12-token continuation was recorded **at every position** of the recorded truth (257 contexts per prompt, batched, bf16) and replayed in `sim.simulate` with the multi-patch rule of the drafting task (byte **and** flag must match, at most 8 patches per round). Two context modes (`raw`; `healed` = the trailing word fragment is held back and the continuation must start with it, otherwise the raw one is used) and two flag modes (`cut` = the task's protocol: cut to K bytes, tokenize context + draft, drop the last token; `full` = tokenize the context + the whole continuation, drop only its last token, then cut to K; its flags are those of the longer text, so it is slightly more optimistic). OLMo 2: the same continuations, draft = dolma2 tokens of the draft text without its last token, K in tokens = K/4, acceptance on the recorded OLMo truth (72 tokens).

Emitted bytes per round, multi-patch, extended set, 256 new bytes (full table with all modes and the within-patch rule: `sim.md`):

| drafter | flags | K=4 | K=8 | K=12 | K=16 | K=24 | K=32 |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| SmolLM2-135M, healed | cut | 1.43 | 2.32 | 2.93 | 3.32 | 3.74 | 4.06 |
| SmolLM2-135M, healed | full | 2.31 | 3.01 | 3.44 | 3.75 | 4.01 | 4.19 |
| SmolLM2-360M, raw | cut | 1.37 | 2.31 | 2.94 | 3.37 | 3.88 | 4.38 |
| SmolLM2-360M, raw | full | 2.27 | 3.04 | 3.49 | 3.83 | 4.32 | **4.52** |
| Qwen2.5-0.5B, healed | full | 2.30 | 3.17 | 3.58 | 3.86 | 4.34 | 4.46 |
| combined (context + n-gram), dolma2 flags | cut | 1.87 | 2.95 | 3.42 | 3.68 | 3.82 | 3.87 |

Within-patch rule with the same continuations saturates at 2.4-2.5 bytes per round. Bytes per global pass at K = 32: 11.1 (135M), 11.9 (360M), 12.6 (Qwen) against 3.79 per patch without drafting and 7.3 for the combined drafter. Only about **25%** of rounds cross a boundary (24-28% for the LMs, 35% for the combined drafter). Default prompts (reported only): 4.14 / 4.46 / 4.57 at K = 32. Larger drafters help little (135M -> 360M -> 500M: 4.19 / 4.47 / 4.46 at K = 32): the limit is that one wrong token ends the round, and a small LM predicts the exact next word of a model that is itself a 1B model only about one time in two. **OLMo 2** with the same continuations: 2.98 / 3.19 / 3.90 tokens per round at 8 tokens (10.9 / 11.7 / 14.3 bytes per round). The token model profits more than Bolmo from the same drafter, because it needs no flag to match and no byte-level agreement.

**Drafter cost (B0.4, `tools/cross_drafter_cost.py`, `cost_*.json`).** A drafter round in one CUDA graph (static cache of 512, context 200 tokens, catch-up over c = 3 tokens, then M - 1 single-token steps, bf16, median of 5 trials of 40 replays): SmolLM2-135M **3.7 ms** for one token, +3.7 ms per further token (M = 4: 15.6, M = 8: 30.2, M = 12: 45.0 ms); SmolLM2-360M 5.5 ms per token (M = 8: 44.3); Qwen2.5-0.5B 5.1 ms per token (M = 8: 40.8). A Bolmo round is 1.4-2.0 ms. The code is HF's eager forward captured in a graph (as for OLMo), so these numbers are launch-bound (about 100 us per layer for hidden size 576); even the weight-bandwidth floor of the smallest model is 0.6 ms per token (0.27 GB at 448 GB/s), and a draft needs 3-10 tokens per round. The first attempt (full grid) ran into my 550 s timeout before writing its file and Qwen's sliding-window mask dict broke the first Qwen run; both were fixed and rerun with a smaller grid.

**Checkpoint B0:** H34 supported, H36 refuted.

### Stage B1: cost model, break-even, gate (`tools/cross_costmodel.py`, `results/crossing/b1/costmodel.{md,json}`)

Round cost = drafter graph (M = p90 of the tokens a round needs, c = 3) + local verify pair(K) from Task A's best path (`results/parallel/bench/<prec>-fused-L0`) + global pass B(P) + (C_p - C_n) for the P patches the round runs speculatively + outside-replays per round + measured host time of the drafter (about 0.1 ms). New measurement: the 16 global layers over P patches in one pass, P up to 8 (`tools/draft_global_multi_bench.py --max-p 8`; bf16 7.69 / 7.79 / 7.80 / 8.22 / 8.26 / 8.26 / 8.30 / 8.33 ms for P = 1..8, int4 3.76 / 3.83 / 3.86 / 3.93 / 4.07 / 4.31 / 4.57 / 4.84 ms). The K = 0 loop of this model reproduces Task A's model within 0.3% (bf16 default 3.987 vs 3.995; int4 2.006 vs 2.021). Left out (optimistic for multi-patch): the per-position patch-vector select of a multi-patch decoder, and truncation of drafts that need more tokens than the graph's M.

Predicted ms/byte, bf16, default prompts (speed workload), cell chosen on the extended set:

| choice | cell | ms/byte | vs best within-patch drafting (combined, fused, 3.386) |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| multi-patch, any drafter | combined + dolma2 flags, K = 32 | 3.178 | **+6.1%** |
| multi-patch, token LM, measured drafter | SmolLM2-135M, K = 4 | 10.571 | -212% |
| multi-patch, token LM, drafter at bandwidth floor | SmolLM2-135M, K = 32 | 4.521 | -33.5% |
| multi-patch, token LM, free drafter (not realizable) | SmolLM2-360M, K = 32 | 2.628 | +22.4% |
| within-patch, token LM, free drafter | Qwen2.5-0.5B, K = 12 | 3.316 | +2.1% |

int4 (default 1.683 for the best within-patch drafting): multi-patch with the combined drafter 1.971 (-17%, its K = 16 cell chosen on the extended set; on the extended set itself +1.8%), the best token-LM cell is 8.23 ms/byte with the measured drafter (-389%), 3.57 at the bandwidth floor (-112%) and, with a free drafter, 2.04 (-21%): worse than the minimal loop's 2.0 only because crossing needs the global pass in nearly every round at 3.9 ms. (The int4 numbers use continuations recorded on the all-int4 truth, whose text differs from the bf16 text on all 30 prompts.) Within-patch drafting is capped by the patch length (2.4-2.5 bytes per round), so the free-drafter gain of +22% in bf16 is the whole prize of crossing drafts with a perfect-cost 360M drafter, and it disappears at int4.

OLMo 2 bf16 (verify graph 9.27 -> 10.01 ms for 0 -> 8 tokens, prefill share calibrated on its minimal-loop cell; the same model gives 1.369 for the measured combined K = 8 cell, 1.419 measured, -3.5%): with the LM drafters the best cell is Qwen2.5-0.5B at K = 8 bytes, **3.04 ms/byte, slower than the 2.68 minimal loop** (the drafter costs 15.8 ms per round against a 9.4 ms verify). A two-point calibration gave a negative host time, so I used one point and report the 3.5% error.

**Gate: not met.** The best predicted Bolmo gain over the best within-patch drafting is +6.1% with the non-LM combined drafter (limit 10%) and negative for every token-LM drafter, so Stage B2 was **not built** and H38 is not tested.

### Report

**1.** Hypotheses copied before any measurement (prefix of this file diffed against `results/FINDINGS.before_crossing.md`: unchanged); verdicts above. **2.** Flag agreement tables and disagreement kinds above. **3.** Acceptance by drafter x K for Bolmo and OLMo 2 and the drafter cost above. **4.** Cost model, break-even and predictions above; the gate decision is "stop". **5.** Not built (gate).

**6. Can drafting reduce the byte tax, and what does it take?** Not with a small token LM on this machine. The acceptance side is better than I feared and worse than I predicted: Bolmo's flags follow dolma2 closely (98.7% of positions, 95% of patches exactly), so re-tokenizing a drafted text does give usable flags, and crossing drafts do amortize the global model (11-12 bytes per global pass against 3.8). But a 135M-500M LM reaches only 4.2-4.5 bytes per round, crossing a boundary in 25% of rounds, and its drafts cost 15-60 ms per round as an eager graph, 8-30 times a Bolmo round; at its weight-bandwidth floor it would still cost 2-6 ms per round. The break-even is lower than I thought (about one patch per round, because the global pass is nearly flat in P), and even a free 360M drafter would give +22% in bf16 and nothing in int4, while the same drafter helps OLMo 2 more (14 bytes per round) and so would widen the byte tax once it is affordable. What it would take: a drafter that costs well under 1 ms per round (not a 100M-parameter LM at 448 GB/s, but a lookup or a tiny model fused with the Bolmo graph), and a higher per-token accuracy than a 135M-500M LM has (about 4.5 bytes per round means one miss every 1-2 tokens). The cheaper levers stay the ones from the earlier tasks: int4 or int8 for the global model, and the combined lookup drafter, with at most +6% (bf16) from drafting across patches.

### Deviations, limits, problems

- Correction made during the work: my first int4 predictions replayed continuations recorded on the bf16 text against the int4 truth (the two texts differ on every prompt, so acceptance was about zero). I noticed it from an implausible 1.07 bytes per round, re-recorded the three drafters on the int4 truth (`run_cross_int4.sh`) and reran the model; the table above is the corrected one. The bf16 results were not affected.
- Stage B2 not built (gate). No new GPU loop and no speed table; everything in B1 is a prediction.
- The drafter is HF's eager forward in a manually captured graph, not a tuned kernel; the cost is launch-bound. The bandwidth-floor row is the sensitivity for that, the free-drafter row for the acceptance effect only.
- Continuations were recorded for the 256-byte truth only (the 1024-byte truth and an OLMo 288-token truth were not used for the LMs). `cut` and `full` flag protocols and the `raw`/`healed` modes are all reported; I did not pick the most favourable one for a verdict (H36 is refuted under every mode). The drafters, K and modes were chosen on the extended set; the default prompts were only reported. The extended set is also where H36 is evaluated, as the task defines it, so the best-cell numbers are slightly optimistic.
- The "full" flag mode uses flags from a longer text than the cut draft; it is not the task's literal protocol and is labelled as such.
- Draft token counts per round (p90) fix M in the cost model; truncation of the rarer longer drafts is ignored. The break-even model assumes within-patch drafting emits min(b, 3.79) per round and a multi-patch draft of K bytes spans 1 + K/3.79 patches.
- OLMo's cost model has one calibration point (see above) and an assumed 0.11 ms of drafter host time.
- Tests: 11 new offline tests (`tests/test_crossing_flags.py`; flag agreement, the draft protocol with a fake tokenizer, the multi-patch replay in the simulator, two with the real tokenizer if cached); five injected bugs (keep the last token, flags shifted by one, final token evaluated, patch exactness by end only, no check of the held-back fragment) are each caught. 180 tests pass. Nothing was committed to git.
- New files: `byteprof/drafting/{flags,lmdraft}.py`, `tools/cross_*.py`, `run_cross_cost.sh`, `tests/test_crossing_flags.py`, `results/crossing/`.

## Drafter diagnostics

Task: `TASK_drafter_diagnostics.md`. Builds on `## Crossing drafts`.

### Pre-registered hypotheses and prediction (copied verbatim before any analysis)

The text below, from the task file, is not edited.

Prediction for the record: most of the gap is text (cause 1); protocol
differences account for less than a third of it.

- **H39 (protocol):** evaluating the same drafter continuations against the
  **same** reference text under Bolmo's rule and under OLMo's rule, the bytes
  per round differ by less than 1.3x.
- **H40 (text):** the drafters' top-1 next-token agreement is at least 15
  percentage points higher on OLMo's output than on Bolmo's.
- **H41 (round ends):** in Bolmo's multi-patch simulation, fewer than 10% of
  rounds end on a wrong boundary flag while the bytes are right.

### Verdicts at a glance

| Hypothesis | Verdict | Numbers |
|---|---|---|
| **H39** protocol | **Refuted** | the same recorded continuations, the same reference text, K = 32 bytes / 8 tokens: the token rule gives **2.7-3.3x** the bytes per round of the byte rule (18 drafter x context x flag cells; limit 1.3x). SmolLM2-360M raw, `cut`: Bolmo text 13.13 vs 4.38 (3.00x), OLMo text 11.62 vs 3.95 (2.94x) |
| **H40** text | **Refuted** | top-1 next-token agreement (task definition: bytes up to the shorter length) is **not higher** on OLMo's output: SmolLM2-360M 72.9% (OLMo text) vs 77.7% (Bolmo text), -4.8 points; 135M -5.4; Qwen2.5-0.5B 80.3% vs 79.8%, +0.5. Limit: +15 points |
| **H41** round ends | **Refuted (near miss)** | rounds that end on a wrong boundary flag with the right byte: **10.8-11.9%** of Bolmo's multi-patch rounds (SmolLM2-360M raw `cut` 11.1%; German prose 18-22%), limit < 10% |
| Prediction "most of the gap is text, protocol < 1/3" | **Wrong** | the text contributes 0.89x to 1.03x (OLMo's text is slightly *harder* for the SmolLM drafters, equal for Qwen); the protocol and the granularity of matching explain 3.0x on their own |

The premise in the task's "Why" section ("the drafter agrees with Bolmo's next token about 50% of the time but with OLMo's about 75-80%") does not hold. The per-token agreement is the same for both models' text (72-80%, table 4). The 50% came from reading Bolmo's 4.5 bytes per round as 4.5 *tokens*' worth of acceptance; the two numbers count different units (below).

### 1. Simulator audit (analysis 1)

Both simulators replay the same recorded continuations (`tools/cross_sim_report.py`, `byteprof/drafting/{sim,tokens,lmdraft}.py`). Side by side:

| item | Bolmo (byte rule, `sim.simulate`) | OLMo 2 (token rule, `tokens.simulate_tokens`) | asymmetry | intended? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| cut a draft, drop its last token | continuation cut to K bytes, context + draft tokenized **together** with dolma2, last (possibly incomplete) token dropped (`cut`); `full` tokenizes the whole 12-token continuation and drops only its last token | draft text tokenized **alone**, last token dropped, at most 8 tokens | in-context vs stand-alone tokenization; the first draft token can differ in the stand-alone case when it continues a word (never at a token boundary, where OLMo's rounds start) | yes; no sign of an effect: the same function applied to Bolmo's text ends the token rounds on a wrong token as often (77% vs 80%) |
| K | 32 bytes | 8 tokens (`K_TOK[32]`), about 29 bytes of 3.6-byte tokens | 8 tokens are a little shorter than 32 bytes | yes; if anything it caps OLMo more: 3-4% of the byte rounds end at the K cap (1.4-2.6%) or the patch cap, 12.8-13.3% of the token rounds end at 8 matched tokens (section 2) |
| bonus (model-predicted) unit | **one byte** | **one token**, 4.0-4.4 bytes per round | **the unit** | yes (each model emits its own unit), but it is the biggest effect (table 3) |
| where a round starts | after any byte (68% of rounds start **inside** a dolma2 token) | always on a token end | the drafter's context | a consequence of the unit; the second biggest effect (tables 3 and 6) |
| what must match | byte and boundary flag, at most 8 patches | token id | granularity | yes |
| rounds, cap, end of generation | `emitted = 1 + L`, cut at the end of the 256 recorded bytes | `emitted = 1 + L` tokens, cut at 72 tokens (about 257 bytes) | none material | yes |
| drafter context | prompt + accepted output; `raw` = as is, `healed` = trailing word fragment held back | the same recordings of the same contexts at token ends | none | yes |
| **token healing (`healed`)** | holds back the fragment **without its leading space** | same | the drafter's context ends in a bare space, which BPE models rarely continue; the merge then fell back to the raw continuation, so `healed` was almost identical to `raw` in Task B (360M: 4.34 vs 4.38) | **no: a weakness of Task B's healing** (fixed below) |
| recordings | Task B batch recording of 30 prompts | the same, plus the new byte-level recording of OLMo's text | batched bf16 generation rounds differently with another batch composition | 13% of the re-recorded OLMo items differ from Task B's in some byte of the 12-token continuation (5% within the first 12 bytes, 0.3% in the first byte); effect on bytes per round about 1% (OLMo token cell 11.62 vs 11.71) |

**Cross-simulator agreement test:** `tests/test_diagnostics.py::test_byte_and_token_rule_agree_when_every_token_is_one_flagged_byte` runs both rules on one hand-made case in which every token is one flagged byte; rounds, drafts offered, accepted lengths and emitted counts are identical (K = 3 and 6, wrong token at position 1, 2, 4), and `test_token_rule_credits_the_bonus_token_in_bytes` shows the one intended difference. Both agree with `sim.simulate` / `simulate_tokens` (`test_replay_byte_rounds_reproduces_simulate`, ...). The round-end classifier is tested kind by kind (`test_classify_end_each_kind`, 13 tests in the file; five injected bugs and two further ones in the align/patch-limit code are each caught).

**Fixed asymmetry and corrected Task B numbers.** New `healed2` mode holds back the fragment *with* its leading space (`diagnostics.context_back_space`; a lone trailing space too), and `tools/diag_record_healed2.py` re-recorded all three drafters on both texts. Next-byte agreement inside a token rises from 25-30% to 67-73%. Effect on the extended set at K = 32 bytes (Task B's table cells, old -> corrected):

| drafter | flags | Task B (best of raw/healed) | healed2 |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| SmolLM2-135M | `cut` / `full` | 4.06 / 4.19 | **4.24 / 4.29** |
| SmolLM2-360M | `cut` / `full` | 4.38 / **4.52** (Task B's best cell) | **4.59 / 4.62** |
| Qwen2.5-0.5B | `cut` / `full` | 4.29 / 4.46 | **4.54 / 4.54** |

Healing at an OLMo token end (it holds back the last token and makes the drafter regenerate it) lowers OLMo's bytes per round by 3-4% (360M 11.62 -> 11.21, Qwen 14.20 -> 13.79), so OLMo's Task B cells stand as its best. The corrected best Bolmo cell is 4.62 bytes per round (was 4.52): H36 stays refuted (limit 5). The 3x gap is unchanged (14.2 / 4.62 = 3.07 across the two best cells, 13.79 / 4.54 = 3.04 for Qwen healed2 on both sides).

No other asymmetry was unintended; nothing else in Task B changes.

### 2. Why rounds end (analysis 2; `cut`, SmolLM2-360M raw = Task B's best cell; all drafters and modes in `results/diagnostics/report.md`)

| text, rule | rounds | wrong byte / token | right byte, wrong flag | drafter exhausted | cap (K) | patch cap (8) | end of generation | hit rate p | p/(1-p) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Bolmo text, byte rule | 1462 | 77.1% | **11.1%** | 6.0% | 1.4% | 2.9% | 1.6% | 0.794 | 3.85 |
| OLMo text, byte rule | 1594 | 82.1% | 7.7% | 3.7% | 1.8% | 3.5% | 1.3% | 0.767 | 3.29 |
| Bolmo text, token rule | 475 | 77.1% | n/a | 5.9% | 12.8% | n/a | 4.2% | 0.772 | 3.39 |
| OLMo text, token rule | 541 | 80.2% | n/a | 3.3% | 13.3% | n/a | 3.1% | 0.733 | 2.74 |

(Token rule: a wrong token is the only way to be rejected, there are no flags; its cap is 8 tokens.) By prompt type for Bolmo's text (wrong byte / wrong flag / exhausted): English 88% / 7% / 1%, Python 85% / 7% / 0%, German 75% / **22%** / 1%, JSON 75% / 13% / 6%, Unicode mix 61% / 7% / **22%** (the drafter proposes nothing: continuation does not start with the held-back fragment or ends inside a multi-byte character). Qwen2.5-0.5B and SmolLM2-135M show the same split (wrong flag 10.8-11.9% for all). The per-unit hit rate is **0.79 per byte** for Bolmo (rounds end at the first miss: 1 + p/(1-p) = 4.85 against 4.38 measured) and **0.73-0.77 per token** for the token rule; the hit rate per unit is about the same, but a token unit carries 3.6 bytes and a byte unit one.

### 3. Same text, both protocols (analysis 3, H39)

Bytes emitted per round, extended set, K = 32 bytes / 8 tokens, `cut` flags. Bolmo's byte rule on OLMo's text uses recordings at every byte of OLMo's output (new, `tools/diag_record_olmo_bytes.py`), flags from OLMo's own tokens; the token rule on Bolmo's text uses the dolma2 retokenization of Bolmo's output as the reference tokens.

| drafter (context) | Bolmo text, byte rule | Bolmo text, token rule | OLMo text, byte rule | OLMo text, token rule | token/byte, Bolmo text | token/byte, OLMo text | text effect, byte rule (OLMo/Bolmo text) | text effect, token rule |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| SmolLM2-135M (healed, Task B) | 4.06 | 12.23 | 3.79 | 10.98 | 3.01x | 2.90x | 0.93x | 0.90x |
| SmolLM2-360M (raw, Task B) | 4.38 | 13.13 | 3.95 | 11.62 | 3.00x | 2.94x | 0.90x | 0.89x |
| Qwen2.5-0.5B (healed, Task B) | 4.29 | 13.82 | 4.55 | 14.20 | 3.22x | 3.12x | 1.06x | 1.03x |
| SmolLM2-360M (healed2) | 4.59 | 12.65 | 4.16 | 11.21 | 2.76x | 2.70x | 0.91x | 0.89x |
| Qwen2.5-0.5B (healed2) | 4.54 | 13.64 | 4.98 | 13.79 | 3.01x | 2.77x | 1.10x | 1.01x |

The Task B cells are reproduced (4.38, 4.52 `full`, 4.06, 4.29; OLMo 10.98-14.2, within 1% of the old recording). **The rows (text) differ by 0.89-1.10x; the columns (rule) by 2.7-3.3x.** The gap is the rule, not the text.

**The rule, step by step on the same text** (bytes per round, `cut`; "aligned" is a what-if, not a Bolmo rule: a byte round that stops inside a token also emits the rest of that token, as a token model's bonus token does, so every round starts on a token end; "accepted only" is the token rule without its bonus token):

| drafter (context), text | byte + flags (Bolmo) | bytes only | aligned byte | token, accepted only | token rule (OLMo) | bonus token, bytes/round |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 360M raw, Bolmo text | 4.38 | 4.59 | 11.87 | 8.91 | 13.13 | 4.21 |
| 360M raw, OLMo text | 3.95 | 3.94 | 11.33 | 7.59 | 11.62 | 4.03 |
| Qwen healed, Bolmo text | 4.29 | 4.29 | 12.38 | 9.43 | 13.82 | 4.39 |
| Qwen healed, OLMo text | 4.55 | 4.45 | 13.41 | 10.05 | 14.20 | 4.14 |
| 135M healed, Bolmo text | 4.06 | 4.19 | 10.98 | 8.06 | 12.23 | 4.17 |
| 135M healed, OLMo text | 3.79 | 3.81 | 10.92 | 6.93 | 10.98 | 4.04 |

Other reference variants: Bolmo text with the flags of the dolma2 retokenization instead of Bolmo's own (360M raw `cut`): 4.44 against 4.38.

### 4. Next-token agreement (analysis 4, H40)

At every dolma2 token end of each model's output, the drafter's top-1 next token (first token of the recorded raw continuation) against the model's actual next token; "agree" = identical bytes up to the shorter length (the task's definition), "exact" = identical bytes. Next byte: the drafter's first byte against the next byte at all byte positions.

| drafter | model's text | tokens | agree | exact | next byte, ctx on a token end | next byte, ctx inside a token (raw / healed2) |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| SmolLM2-135M | Bolmo | 1694 | 76.9% | 54.2% | 87.9% | 29.4% / 72.4% |
| SmolLM2-135M | OLMo 2 | 1714 | 71.5% | 53.6% | 85.8% | 24.3% / 67.1% |
| SmolLM2-360M | Bolmo | 1694 | 77.7% | 54.7% | 89.1% | 29.4% / 73.4% |
| SmolLM2-360M | OLMo 2 | 1714 | 72.9% | 55.1% | 86.1% | 24.8% / 69.2% |
| Qwen2.5-0.5B | Bolmo | 1694 | 79.8% | 72.3% | 93.8% | 24.2% / 69.7% |
| Qwen2.5-0.5B | OLMo 2 | 1714 | 80.3% | 75.7% | 93.5% | 20.9% / 69.9% |

By type the models differ more than overall: SmolLM2-360M agrees on 71.1% / 80.5% / 69.9% / 86.0% / 77.8% (English / Python / German / JSON / Unicode) of Bolmo's tokens and on 78.3% / 80.8% / **48.6%** / 78.3% / 74.1% of OLMo's (German is the weakness of the small drafters on OLMo's German text). Whether the context ends on a token end matters far more than which model wrote the text: **87-94% at a token end against 21-29% inside a token (raw)**, and 67-73% inside a token with `healed2`.

### 5. Where the first miss falls (analysis 5)

Byte rule, rounds that end on a wrong byte (360M raw `cut`; the other drafters are within 2 points):

| text | wrong-byte rounds | miss on the first draft byte | word start | inside a word | whitespace / punctuation | inside a multi-byte character | whitespace-only near miss |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Bolmo | 1127 | 68.9% | 19.3% | 65.7% | 9.9% | 5.1% | 2.0% |
| OLMo 2 | 1308 | 70.6% | 21.3% | 66.8% | 9.1% | 2.8% | 1.5% |

Near misses are rare: the draft differs from the truth only in whitespace in 0.5-2.0% of the wrong-byte rounds, and a right-byte-wrong-flag round (token boundary shift) is 11% of all rounds. Ignoring the flags altogether (bytes only) would raise Bolmo's bytes per round by 5% (360M: 4.38 -> 4.59), 3% (135M), 0% (Qwen). A looser rule would therefore not close much of the gap.

**Where the round starts is what matters.** Bolmo text, byte rule (360M raw): rounds that start on a token end are 32% of all rounds and emit **9.81** bytes; rounds that start inside a token are 68% and emit **1.83** (OLMo text: 9.10 and 1.60). The "aligned" what-if above puts every round on a token end and reaches 11.87 (accepted draft bytes per round 8.62 instead of 3.39; the rest of the token adds 2.25 bytes). With `healed2` the rounds inside a token emit 2.48 instead of 1.83 bytes: the first-byte accuracy at an arbitrary position is 73%, but rounds start right after a miss, where the drafter has just been wrong, and 69% of the wrong-byte rounds fail on the very first draft byte.

### 6. Text predictability (analysis 6)

Teacher-forced bits per byte of each model's output under each drafter (bf16, the tokens that start after the prompt, extended set; `tools/diag_ce.py`):

| drafter | Bolmo text | OLMo text |
|---|---:|---:|
| SmolLM2-135M | 0.479 | 0.484 |
| SmolLM2-360M | 0.420 | 0.428 |
| Qwen2.5-0.5B | 0.426 | 0.365 |

The texts are about equally predictable (by type: Python 0.29-0.32 vs 0.16-0.19, German 0.56-0.69 vs 0.57-0.94, English 0.41-0.47 vs 0.38-0.42, JSON, Unicode mix equal within 0.1). Repetition (8-byte windows of the output seen earlier in prompt + output): Bolmo **49.6%** (3175 of 6400 windows), OLMo 2 **43.2%** (2717 of 6289); Bolmo's output is the more repetitive one, the opposite of the "OLMo's text is more predictable" explanation.

### 7. Decomposition of the 3x gap (point 4 of the report)

Gap = OLMo's best cell (token rule on OLMo's text) / Bolmo's best cell (byte rule on Bolmo's text), same drafter, Task B cells:

| drafter | gap | text | protocol and granularity (product) | = flags | x bonus unit and round alignment | x matching granularity (byte vs token) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| SmolLM2-360M raw | 2.65x | 0.89x | 3.00x | 1.05x | 2.59x | 1.11x |
| Qwen2.5-0.5B healed | 3.31x | 1.03x | 3.22x | 1.00x | 2.89x | 1.12x |
| SmolLM2-135M healed | 2.70x | 0.90x | 3.01x | 1.03x | 2.62x | 1.11x |

(Text = token-rule bytes per round on OLMo's text / on Bolmo's text; flags = byte + flags -> bytes only on Bolmo's text; bonus unit and alignment = bytes only -> aligned byte rule; granularity = aligned byte rule -> token rule.) In logarithmic terms for SmolLM2-360M: text -13%, flags +5%, bonus unit and round alignment +98%, granularity +10% of the gap. The 2.59x of the bonus unit and alignment splits, order-dependently, into about 1.5x from the free bytes that finish the token (2.25 bytes per round on top of the accepted ones) and about 1.7x from the rounds restarting on a token end, where the drafter is right 86-94% of the time against 25-30% inside a token.

### 8. Consequences (point 5 of the report)

- **Explanation in Task B was wrong.** "No flags to match" explains 0-5% of the gap, not most of it (flags agree with dolma2 on 98.7% of bytes and cost 5% of the bytes per round). The gap is that a token model (a) emits a whole token (about 4 bytes) as its own bonus and (b) always resumes on a token end, where a 135M-500M LM predicts the next token about as well as it does for Bolmo's text; Bolmo's byte rounds mostly restart in the middle of a token, right after a miss.
- **H36 / H37:** H36 stays refuted: the corrected best Bolmo cell is 4.62 bytes per round (`full`, 360M healed2), 4.59 for the task's `cut` protocol; the limit is 5. H37 stays refuted: the cost model was not rerun (offline, but the acceptance change is +2-5%); the best token-LM cell was 212% slower than within-patch drafting at a drafter cost of 15-60 ms per round against 1.4-2.0 ms for a Bolmo round, and +5% acceptance does not change that sign; the free-drafter bound (+22.4%) would rise by a few points (not recomputed).
- **The 3.5x byte-tax prediction (H38, not tested)** rests on OLMo gaining 3x more bytes per round than Bolmo from the same drafter. That number is now explained and it stands, but for a structural reason, not a text or flag reason: it comes with the protocol (token bonus and token-aligned restarts), so it does not shrink with a better drafter; only a protocol change on Bolmo's side would. I did not recompute the 3.48 (it came from the cost model's 10.57 vs 3.04 ms/byte); the +2-5% shift of the Bolmo cells and the 1% recording noise are small against a 3x structural factor.
- **What a byte model could do with this (not built):** a Bolmo drafter that always drafts from the last token end (re-drafting the partial token, i.e. `healed2` done properly: the drafted text starts at the token boundary and the verified prefix is skipped) already recovers first-byte accuracy 25-30% -> 67-73%, yet gains only 5% per round, because rounds start right after a miss. The upper bound from aligning rounds to token ends is the "aligned" what-if (2.6x), and it is not reachable without generating the rest of the token in the same pass.

### Deviations, limits, problems

- GPU use beyond a "few minutes": OLMo-text recordings at every byte (3 runs of 94-113 s), the `healed2` re-recordings (3 runs of 149-185 s), and the cross-entropy runs (3 short runs); no decode loop, no speed run. The `healed2` recordings are an audit fix, not a missing recording; I judged that the task's "fix any unintended asymmetry" covers them, and I report both the Task B and the corrected numbers.
- The first `healed2` recording started with a regex bug (`$` also matches before a trailing newline, `\Z` is correct) that its test caught; I stopped that job, deleted its output and recorded again. The first CE run failed on a token that ends inside a UTF-8 character (OLMo's t0) and was rerun after the fix.
- Bolmo's rounds on OLMo's text start at `prompt + t0`; its flags are OLMo's own token ends. The token rule on Bolmo's text uses the dolma2 retokenization of Bolmo's output (tokens that start at or after the prompt end, last token dropped); a token that spans the prompt end is skipped.
- The `aligned` rule is a what-if used only to attribute the effect of the bonus unit and of the restart position; it is not a protocol either model uses.
- The first-miss classification looks at the position of the wrong byte only; "near miss" is the whitespace-only variant defined in `byteprof/drafting/diagnostics.py::ws_near_miss`, other kinds of near misses (partial words, synonyms) are not separated.
- All numbers use the extended set (25 prompts) at 256 new bytes (OLMo: 72 tokens), as in Task B; the cost model was not rerun; the OLMo token-boundary items are the new recordings (equal to Task B's within 1% in bytes per round).
- Tests: `tests/test_diagnostics.py`, 13 tests (191 + 2 pass in the full suite: 193 total); injected bugs caught: flag/byte confusion in the round-end classifier, cap vs exhausted, bonus-token bytes, align off-by-one, patch limit ignored. New files: `byteprof/drafting/diagnostics.py` (one optional argument `back_fn` added to `lmdraft.record_continuations`), `tools/diag_{record_olmo_bytes,record_healed2,analyze,ce,report}.py`, `run_diag_{record,healed2,analyze,ce}.sh`, `results/diagnostics/` (full tables: `report.md`). Nothing was committed to git. Backup of FINDINGS.md before this section: `results/FINDINGS.before_diagnostics.md`.

## Patch-aligned rounds

CPU-only simulation and cost model (TASK_patch_aligned.md). Hypotheses were registered before any analysis; results and verdicts follow them.

### Pre-registered hypotheses and predictions (copied verbatim from TASK_patch_aligned.md before any analysis)

Copy this section **verbatim** into a new `## Patch-aligned rounds` section of
`FINDINGS.md` **before** any analysis. Never edit it afterwards. Mark each
supported, refuted or not tested, with numbers.

Predictions for the record: hybrid drafts lift acceptance clearly, but drafter
cost still decides; the lookup-only gated policy is the only one that can win in
practice, by a few percent over P1; a two-state model explains bytes per round
well.

- **H42 (healed2 loose end):** with `healed2`, rounds that start mid-token
  still emit less than half the bytes of rounds that start at a token end, and
  most of the shortfall comes from the short remaining token and the one-byte
  free unit, not from drafter accuracy.
- **H43 (cost model rerun):** with the `healed2` acceptance rates, the best
  token-LM crossing cell at the drafter's bandwidth floor is still at least 20%
  slower than P0 in bf16, and the predicted byte tax with both models on the
  same LM drafter stays at least 3.0x.
- **H44 (hybrid acceptance):** P2 reaches at least 6.0 emitted bytes per round
  for the best LM drafter (Task B best: 4.62 with `healed2`).
- **H45 (gated LM):** P3 with the drafter at its bandwidth floor is predicted at
  least 5% faster than P0 in bf16; with the measured drafter cost it is slower.
- **H46 (gated lookup):** P4 is predicted at least 8% faster than P0 in bf16.
- **H47 (two-state model):** a model with two restart states (token boundary,
  mid-token), their transition probabilities, and the mean bytes per round in
  each state reproduces the simulated bytes per round within ±10% for every
  policy and both models.

### Verdicts at a glance

| Hypothesis | Verdict | Numbers |
|---|---|---|
| **H42** healed2, mid-token rounds | **Refuted** (first half supported, second half refuted) | With `healed2` mid-token rounds still emit **0.18-0.31x** the bytes of token-end rounds (SmolLM2-360M `cut`: 2.52 vs 9.17 B), so "less than half" holds. But the one-byte free unit plus the short remaining token (mean 2.8-3.0 B left in the token) explain only **21-32%** of the shortfall; **68-79%** is the drafter being wrong at these restarts (first draft byte right in 17-23% of mid-token rounds against 77-86% at token ends) |
| **H43** cost model rerun | **Refuted** (first half supported, second half refuted) | Best token-LM crossing cell at the drafter's bandwidth floor, bf16: **1.30x** the best within-patch (P0) ms/byte on ext (Task B cells: 1.39x ext, 1.34x default as reported); "at least 20% slower" holds. Byte tax with both models on the same LM drafter, ext/ext: **2.89x** measured cost, 2.55x floor (Task B cells recomputed the same way: 3.04x, 2.74x); "stays at least 3.0x" fails. Task B's reported 3.48x mixed Bolmo default prompts with OLMo ext prompts |
| **H44** hybrid acceptance | **Refuted** | P2 reaches at most **5.60** emitted bytes per round (Qwen2.5-0.5B, `raw`/`healed`, `full` flags, K = 32; 5.44 with the `cut` protocol); needed 6.0. Task B's best was 4.62, so P2 lifts acceptance by about 21% over it, not the 30% the line needs. The variant P2b (LM directly at boundaries) reaches 6.3-6.9, but is not the registered policy |
| **H45** gated LM | **Supported** | P3 with the drafter at its bandwidth floor: **+32.6%** ext / **+36.2%** default vs P0 (SmolLM2-135M `raw`, K = 32); with the measured drafter graph **-45.5%** ext / -46.0% default (slower). At the floor it only ties the lookup-only P4 (2.028 vs 2.023 ms/byte), see 4 |
| **H46** gated lookup | **Supported, far above the line** | P4 (K = 32, bf16): **2.023 ms/byte ext, 2.259 default = +32.8% / +33.1%** vs P0 (3.011 / 3.377 at K = 8). int4: +24.0% ext, +8.9% default |
| **H47** two-state model | **Supported in-sample, weak as a predictor** | Fitted on all rounds of a cell, the model reproduces the simulated bytes per round within **-1.3% ... +0.2%** in all 16 cells (Bolmo policies and OLMo 2's text under the byte rule; OLMo's own token rule has one state). That is close to an identity. Fitted on half the prompts and predicted for the other half, the error exceeds 10% in 3 of 16 cells (max 17.2%) |

Predictions for the record: "hybrid drafts lift acceptance clearly, but drafter cost still decides": right (P2 about +21% bytes per round over Task B's best, but slower than P0 in every measured/floor cell). "The lookup-only gated policy is the only one that can win in practice": right. "By a few percent over P1": **wrong by a large margin**; P4 is 29% faster than P1 (2.023 vs 2.854 ms/byte ext), P1 itself being only +5.2% over P0. "A two-state model explains bytes per round well": right in-sample, see 5.

### Deviations and missing recordings

- **`healed2` exists only for the extended set, bf16.** The diagnostics task recorded it for the 25 extended prompts on the bf16 truth. There is no `healed2` recording for the 5 default prompts and none for the int4 truth. The task is CPU only with no new recordings, so no new recording was made. Consequences: `healed2` cells have no default-prompt column ("n/a"); int4 LM cells use `raw`/`healed` only; the byte tax is computed ext/ext. Lookup-only policies (P0, P1, P4) need no recording and have all columns for both precisions.
- K grid: the task's {8, 16, 32} plus Task B's {4, 12, 24}, so that the rerun of Task B's cells (H43) is on the same grid; the tables show 8/16/32, the selections use all six.
- Policy tables use the `cut` flag protocol (flags from tokenizing context + draft cut to K, last token dropped); H43/H44 also report `full` (Task B's optimistic mode) because Task B's cells used it. Lookup drafts get their flags the same way (dolma2 retokenization, +150 us host per round).
- Extra policies beyond the task list, labelled: **L1** (the LM drafts the crossing every round, i.e. Task B's cells; used for the rerun) and **P2b** (P2, but a round that starts at a boundary asks the LM directly). Oracle rows are marked `*` and use a perfect lookup part (true rest of the patch, or true crossing draft), the LM part stays real.
- The LM drafter graph is costed as in Task B (3 catch-up tokens + M - 1 steps, M = p90 of the continuation tokens a call needs, 11 for K = 32). Gating means an LM call follows several lookup rounds and has to catch up on more than 3 tokens; this is not modelled and would make P2/P3 slightly slower.
- Everything here is a prediction of the cost model from recorded rounds and microbenchmarks (Task A fused verify costs, `B(P)` and `C_p - C_n` from Tasks A/B). **No multi-patch loop has been built**; the cost of the multi-patch decoder patch-select and of pooling P patches is not measured (see the sensitivity in 6).
- The Bolmo/OLMo ratio exists for bf16 only (no int4 OLMo model).

### 1. Hypotheses

Copied verbatim above before any analysis; verdicts in the table above.

### 2. The `healed2` decomposition (loose end 1, H42)

`tools/aligned_h42.py` -> `results/aligned/h42_healed2.{md,json}`. Rounds of the byte rule (crossing, K = 32, at most 8 patches) on the 25 extended prompts, split by where the round restarts: on a dolma2 token end, or inside a token with 1, 2, 3, 4+ bytes of it still to come. SmolLM2-360M, `cut` flags (the other five drafter x flag cells are in the md file and agree):

| restart | rounds | B/round | mean L | first byte right | wrong_byte | wrong_flag | cap | exhausted | patch_cap | free unit completes the token: B | oracle in token: B |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| token end | 433 | 9.17 | 8.22 | 80% | 64% | 16% | 3% | 6% | 6% | - | - |
| 1 byte left | 262 | 2.37 | 1.37 | 16% | 73% | 9% | 1% | 16% | 1% | 2.70 | 10.23 |
| 2 left | 238 | 2.73 | 1.73 | 32% | 78% | 11% | 1% | 8% | 2% | 3.75 | 11.37 |
| 3 left | 152 | 1.64 | 0.64 | 20% | 88% | 10% | 0% | 2% | 0% | 3.47 | 12.87 |
| 4+ left | 310 | 2.94 | 1.94 | 23% | 87% | 7% | 3% | 3% | 0% | 6.90 | 15.33 |
| all mid-token | 962 | 2.52 | 1.53 | 22% | 81% | 9% | 1% | 7% | 1% | 4.44 | 12.57 |

All three drafters, both flag modes:

| drafter | flags | all rounds B | restart mid-token | B/round token end | B/round mid-token | ratio | first byte right (end / mid) | free unit closes (share of shortfall) | left to accuracy | oracle in token adds |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|
| SmolLM2-135M | cut | 4.24 | 69% | 8.09 | 2.51 | 0.31 | 77% / 23% | 1.80 B (32%) | 3.78 B | +8.86 B |
| SmolLM2-135M | full | 4.29 | 68% | 8.43 | 2.35 | 0.28 | 78% / 22% | 1.74 B (29%) | 4.34 B | +9.21 B |
| SmolLM2-360M | cut | 4.59 | 69% | 9.17 | 2.52 | 0.28 | 80% / 22% | 1.91 B (29%) | 4.74 B | +10.05 B |
| SmolLM2-360M | full | 4.62 | 69% | 9.44 | 2.41 | 0.26 | 80% / 22% | 1.89 B (27%) | 5.14 B | +10.42 B |
| Qwen2.5-0.5B | cut | 4.54 | 67% | 9.63 | 2.03 | 0.21 | 86% / 18% | 1.79 B (24%) | 5.81 B | +10.38 B |
| Qwen2.5-0.5B | full | 4.54 | 66% | 9.92 | 1.80 | 0.18 | 86% / 17% | 1.73 B (21%) | 6.39 B | +10.81 B |

(The "all rounds" column reproduces the diagnostics task's healed2 numbers, 4.24-4.62.)

How the shortfall (token-end minus mid-token B/round) is split. *Free unit and remaining token:* a counterfactual one-round replay from each recorded mid-token restart in which the free unit completes the token the last emitted byte is in (the effect of a one-byte instead of a whole-token free unit, capped by the remaining token length; mean remaining 2.8-3.0 B). It closes 1.7-1.9 B, **21-32%** of the 5.6-8.1 B shortfall. *Drafter accuracy:* the rest, 3.8-6.4 B. A second counterfactual, an oracle for the remaining bytes of the patch followed by the real LM continuation at its end, adds 8.9-10.8 B, more than the whole shortfall, so accuracy at these restarts is the lever and the remaining token length is not. The mechanism is the selection effect named in the diagnostics: a round restarts right after a miss, i.e. at a spot where the previous draft was wrong, and the first-byte hit rate there is 17-23% even though the all-position next-byte agreement with `healed2` is 67-73%. Rounds that end on a wrong byte: 81% (mid-token) vs 63-64% (token end). H42's "most of the shortfall comes from the short remaining token and the one-byte free unit" is therefore wrong for `healed2` (it is a minor share, about a quarter), while "mid-token rounds emit less than half" holds.

| drafter cost | Task B contexts (raw, healed) | with healed2 |

Per drafter, `healed2`, measured: 135M 2.52x, 360M 2.44x, Qwen 2.44x (each model on its own best cell). `healed2` helps Bolmo by ~5% and leaves OLMo unchanged (healing at an OLMo token end hurts it), so the tax shrinks slightly. Task B's reported 3.48x divided Bolmo's *default-prompt* ms/byte (10.571) by OLMo's *ext* best (3.041); like-for-like on ext it was 3.04x. A default-prompt `healed2` figure cannot be computed (no recording). Verdict as stated above: the byte tax stays near 3x but falls below the 3.0x line.

### 4. Policy tables

`results/aligned/costmodel.md` has every row (all drafters, cost rows, int4). Round mix = share of rounds that use the crossing rule; B/global = emitted bytes per global-model pass.

**Lookup policies (no recording needed), K chosen on ext; Bolmo/OLMo = Bolmo default-prompt ms/byte over OLMo 2 + combined lookup K = 8 (1.420 measured):**

| precision | policy | K | B/round (ext) | B/global | crossing rounds | ms/byte ext | vs P0 | ms/byte default | vs P0 | Bolmo/OLMo |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| bf16 | P0 within-patch lookup | 8 | 2.30 | 3.8 | 0% | 3.011 | - | 3.377 | - | 2.38 |
| bf16 | P1 crossing lookup every round | 32 | 3.84 | 7.2 | 100% | 2.854 | +5.2% | 3.178 | +5.9% | 2.24 |
| bf16 | **P4 boundary-gated lookup** | 32 | 3.03 | 7.2 | 42% | **2.023** | **+32.8%** | **2.259** | **+33.1%** | **1.59** |
| bf16 | P4* (oracle lookup, upper bound) | 32 | 15.46 | 25.3 | 61% | 0.620 | +79.4% | 0.673 | +80.1% | 0.47 |
| int4 | P0 | 4 | 2.12 | 3.7 | 0% | 1.718 | - | 1.670 | - | n/a |
| int4 | P1 | 16 | 3.57 | 6.6 | 100% | 1.701 | +1.0% | 1.971 | -18.0% | n/a |
| int4 | **P4** | 12 | 2.79 | 6.3 | 44% | **1.306** | **+24.0%** | **1.522** | **+8.9%** | n/a |
| int4 | P4* (oracle, upper bound) | 32 | 14.99 | 24.5 | 61% | 0.489 | +71.6% | 0.479 | +71.3% | n/a |

Why P4 wins: it keeps P0's cheap mid-patch rounds (no global pass; the round ends at the patch end) and spends the global pass only on rounds that start at a boundary, where one pass covers several patches (`B(P)` is nearly flat in P: 7.7 -> 8.3 ms for P = 1..8 in bf16). Bytes per global pass go from 3.8 to 7.2, the global pass being about two thirds of the per-byte time in P0. P1 drafts across boundaries in every round and pays `B(P) + C_p - C_n` in all of them, which is why it gains only 5%. Acceptance alone does not decide it: P4 emits 3.03 B/round, P1 3.84.

**Token-LM policies, bf16, best drafter/context/K per row on ext (`cut`), LM calls per byte and bytes per round in brackets; `vs P0` is on ext, default in brackets where a recording exists:**

| policy | drafter cost: measured graph | at the bandwidth floor | drafter free (not realizable) |
|---|---|---|---|
| L1 (LM every round, Task B protocol) | 9.783 (-225%) [2.86 B, 0.350/B] | 4.117 (-36.7%) [3.43 B, 0.292/B] | 2.421 (+19.6%) [4.44 B, 0.225/B] |
| P2 hybrid | 5.594 (-85.8%) [1.71 B, 0.394/B] | 3.416 (-13.4%; default -17.1%) [4.35 B, 0.225/B] | 2.085 (+30.7%; default +33.5%) [5.44 B, 0.180/B] |
| P2b hybrid, LM direct at boundaries | 5.633 (-87.1%) [1.77 B, 0.439/B] | 3.070 (-2.0%; default +1.1%) [5.84 B, 0.170/B] | 1.735 (+42.4%; default +45.9%) [6.69 B, 0.149/B] |
| P3 boundary-gated LM | 4.381 (-45.5%; default -46.0%) [3.14 B, 0.155/B] | **2.028 (+32.6%; default +36.2%)** [4.61 B, 0.094/B] | 1.262 (+58.1%; default +60.5%) [5.19 B, 0.083/B] |
| P2* oracle completion | 3.024 (-0.4%) | 1.673 (+44.4%; default +47.6%) | 1.016 (+66.3%) |
| P3* oracle completion | 4.248 (-41.1%) | 1.906 (+36.7%; default +40.4%) | 1.155 (+61.6%) |

(135M for the measured and most floor cells, Qwen/360M for several free cells; the drafter per cell is in `costmodel.md`.) Acceptance at K = 32, bf16, ext, bytes per round (cut / full), SmolLM2-360M `raw`: L1 4.24 / 4.38, P2 5.18 / 5.26, P2b 6.32 / 6.49, P3 5.04 / 5.17, P2* 11.87 / 12.38, P3* 6.35 / 6.58; Qwen `raw`: L1 4.16 / 4.27, P2 5.41 / 5.57, P2b 6.67 / 6.83, P3 5.18 / 5.29. `healed2` does not help the hybrids (P2: Qwen 5.39 vs 5.41 raw) because their LM calls sit at token ends, where healing re-generates the last token and lowers accuracy 3-4%. The oracle completion lifts P2 from ~5.4 to ~12 B/round, so lookup completion accuracy is what limits the hybrid.

At the floor, P3 is 2.028 ms/byte against P4's 2.023: the extra acceptance of the LM (10.6 vs 7.2 bytes per global pass) is paid back by its calls (0.094 per byte, p90 11 tokens each). The break-even drafter cost for P3 to beat P4 is about 6.6 ms per call for SmolLM2-135M (the floor is 6.6 ms, the measured HF-eager graph 41 ms); 8.1 ms for 360M (floor 17.8), 9.2 ms for Qwen (floor 24.3). int4 is worse for LM drafters: P3 at the floor is +4.9% ext / -1.2% default, P4 +24% / +8.9%.

### 5. The two-state model (H47)

`tools/aligned_twostate.py`, `byteprof/drafting/aligned.py::two_state_bytes_per_round` and `two_state_figure` (tests in `tests/test_aligned.py`) -> `results/aligned/twostate.{md,json}`. States: the round restarts at a boundary (last emitted atom flagged / dolma2 token end) or mid-patch. The function takes the counts of transitions between the start states of consecutive rounds and the mean bytes per round in each state, and returns the stationary-weighted mean.

| model / policy | drafter | predicted / simulated B/round | error | held-out max error | B/round mid | B/round boundary | boundary share of rounds |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Bolmo P0 K=8 | lookup | 2.292 / 2.297 | -0.2% | 2.0% | 1.76 | 2.65 | 61% |
| Bolmo P1 K=16 | lookup | 3.623 / 3.622 | 0.0% | 4.9% | 3.64 | 3.57 | 25% |
| Bolmo P4 K=16 | lookup | 2.883 / 2.895 | -0.4% | 7.3% | 1.91 | 4.17 | 43% |
| Bolmo P4 K=32 | lookup | 3.020 / 3.035 | -0.5% | 7.7% | 1.87 | 4.61 | 42% |
| Bolmo L1 K=32 | 135M / 360M / Qwen | 4.115 / 4.153; 4.389 / 4.438; 4.368 / 4.426 | -0.9 / -1.1 / -1.3% | 4.2 / 1.3 / 12.0% | 2.99 / 3.10 / 2.69 | 7.23 / 8.08 / 8.80 | 28 / 27 / 28% |
| Bolmo P2 K=32 | 135M / 360M / Qwen | 4.845 / 4.837; 5.083 / 5.071; 5.400 / 5.392 | +0.2% (all) | 1.6 / 3.4 / 7.5% | 5.00 / 5.33 / 5.55 | 4.35 / 4.32 / 4.88 | 25 / 25 / 24% |
| Bolmo P3 K=32 | 135M / 360M / Qwen | 4.444 / 4.491; 4.804 / 4.860; 5.061 / 5.124 | -1.0 / -1.1 / -1.2% | 6.6 / 10.5 / 17.2% | 1.91 / 1.99 / 2.01 | 7.89 / 8.58 / 9.26 | 43 / 44 / 43% |
| OLMo 2 text, byte rule, L1 K=32 | 135M / 360M / Qwen | 3.880, 4.157, 4.983 (equal to simulated) | 0.0% | 2.3 / 8.7 / 5.9% | 2.31 / 2.49 / 2.63 | 7.38 / 7.93 / 9.94 | 31 / 31 / 32% |

"Held-out" = parameters fitted on the even prompts, predicted for the odd ones and the reverse (about 12 prompts per half). The in-sample agreement is essentially an identity (the fitted state means and transitions come from the same rounds), so H47 is supported as a description; as a predictor it holds within 10% in 13 of 16 cells. What it shows: in P0/L1/P3 boundary rounds emit 2-4x as many bytes as mid-patch rounds; in P2 the order is reversed (mid-patch rounds finish the patch by lookup and ask the LM at the boundary, 5.0-5.6 B vs 4.3-4.9 B).

Analytic figure for the second paper (`two_state_figure`: free unit f plus a geometric number of accepted bytes with hit rate h, K = 32; the next round restarts at a boundary with probability q, q = 1 for a token free unit). Parameters from SmolLM2-360M `healed2`: mid-token per-byte hit rate 0.68, 27% of byte rounds restart at a boundary. Bytes per round against the hit rate h_B at a boundary:

| h_B | free unit = 1 byte (q = 0.27) | free unit = 1 token (4 B, q = 1) | ratio |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0.50 | 2.80 | 5.00 | 1.78 |
| 0.70 | 3.16 | 6.33 | 2.00 |
| 0.80 | 3.61 | 8.00 | 2.22 |
| 0.90 | 4.87 | 12.69 | 2.61 |
| 0.95 | 6.65 | 19.32 | 2.90 |

(Full table in `twostate.md`. The analytic version is an illustration with measured parameters; it was not validated against the simulation, only the fitted version above was.)

### 6. Recommendation

**Build P4 (boundary-gated lookup) for bf16; do not build an LM drafter.** P4 is predicted 33% faster than the best within-patch lookup on both prompt sets in bf16 (2.259 vs 3.377 ms/byte on the default prompts), with a drafter that exists today (the combined lookup drafter plus dolma2 retokenization for the flags, ~160 us of host time per round, included). Bolmo/OLMo ratio with OLMo's lookup K = 8 baseline falls from 2.38 (P0) to 1.59. int4 clears the bar on the extended set (+24.0%) but not on the default prompts (+8.9%, K chosen on ext), so int4 is a "maybe" until measured.

Robustness (`tools/aligned_sensitivity.py`, `results/aligned/sensitivity.md`): if `B(P)` grows 3x faster in P than measured, bf16 P4 keeps +28%; if every crossing round carries an unmeasured +1 ms (patch select, pooling of P patches), bf16 keeps +28%, at +2 ms +24%; int4 default goes negative at +1 ms.

Where the gain comes from (so the build stays honest): 0.263 -> 0.139 global-model passes per byte, i.e. a lower pass count at about the same pass cost, plus P0's cheap mid-patch rounds untouched. Acceptance is not the point.

Build outline:
1. Round type from the flag of the last emitted atom (`flag_of(a_i)`, already known after the previous verify): flagged -> boundary round, otherwise mid-patch round.
2. Mid-patch rounds: the existing Task A within-patch path (combined lookup, fused local verify at K, no global pass, single-patch accept rule, ends at the patch end).
3. Boundary rounds: lookup draft up to K = 32 bytes with predicted flags (retokenize context + draft with dolma2, `cut` protocol), at most 8 patches; local encoder over the draft, one global pass over the P speculative patches (`B(P)`), local decoder verify with patch assignment, accept the prefix where byte and flag match, roll the global KV cache and the mLSTM states back to the accepted patch. The patch-assignment / pooling cost for P > 1 is the unmeasured piece: **measure one crossing round end to end before the full loop** and compare with the sensitivity above (the build pays off in bf16 as long as the extra cost stays below ~2 ms per crossing round).
4. Check the output against the minimal loop with the usual tolerance for M > 1 rounding near-ties.

What it would take for an LM drafter to matter: P3 only ties P4 at the bandwidth floor and loses in int4, so the drafter would have to produce an 11-token continuation in under ~6.5 ms (0.6 ms/token, no launch overhead) *and* gain from `raw` contexts at boundaries; the measured HF-eager graph is 41 ms for SmolLM2-135M. Better lookup completion would help more: with an oracle completion P2 reaches 11-12 B/round.

Not done / unsure: no `healed2` recording for the default prompts or int4 (rule: CPU only); the multi-patch decoder cost and the catch-up of an LM drafter after gated rounds are not measured; K for P4 was chosen on 25 extended prompts, and on the 5 default prompts the best int4 K differs (K = 8: +10.1%).

Artifacts: `byteprof/drafting/aligned.py`, `tests/test_aligned.py` (17 tests; 10 injected bugs all caught), `tools/aligned_{h42,sim,costmodel,twostate,sensitivity}.py`, `run_aligned_sim.sh`, `results/aligned/` (`h42_healed2.md`, `tallies_*.json`, `costmodel.md`, `twostate.md`, `sensitivity.md`). `python -m pytest -q`: 210 passed.


## Boundary-gated drafting (P4)

Build and measurement of boundary-gated lookup drafting (TASK_build_p4.md). Hypotheses were registered before any measurement; results and verdicts follow them.

### Pre-registered hypotheses and predictions (copied verbatim from TASK_build_p4.md before any measurement)

Copy this section **verbatim** into a new `## Boundary-gated drafting (P4)`
section of `FINDINGS.md` **before** any measurement. Never edit it afterwards.
Mark each supported, refuted or not tested, with numbers.

Predictions for the record: the extra crossing cost is under 1 ms in bf16; P4
lands at 25–33% faster than P0 in bf16 and 0–10% in int4; the ratio to drafted
OLMo 2 ends near 1.6x.

- **H48 (crossing round cost):** the cost of a crossing round not covered by the
  existing model (per-position patch select, pooling P patches, multi-patch
  acceptance and rollback) is at most 1.0 ms in bf16 at K = 32, P ≤ 8.
- **H49 (end-to-end bf16):** on the default prompts at 256 new bytes, P4 is at
  least 25% faster than P0 (same session, the fused verification of Task A).
- **H50 (cost model):** the measured bf16 and int4 ms/byte of P4 lie within
  ±15% of the prediction updated with the Stage 0 measurement.
- **H51 (exactness):** in fp32, P4's output is byte-identical (bytes and flags)
  to the minimal loop for all 30 prompts at 256 and 1024 new bytes, and the CPU
  toy-model test passes for multi-patch rounds.
- **H52 (int4):** P4 is at least 5% faster than P0 in all-int4 on the default
  prompts.
- **H53 (byte tax):** in a same-session block, bf16 P4 Bolmo divided by OLMo 2
  with combined lookup drafting (K = 8) is at most 1.7x.

### Verdicts at a glance

| Hypothesis | Verdict | Numbers |
|---|---|---|
| **H48** crossing-round cost | **Supported, by a factor of ten** | Extra over `A_K + B(P) + C_K`, K in {8, 16, 32}, P = 2..8: bf16 **mean +0.038 ms, max +0.101 ms** (K = 16, P = 3) measured in one session (limit 1.0 ms); against the stored cost-model inputs mean -0.007, max +0.083 ms. All-int4: mean +0.019, max +0.038 ms (stored inputs: mean +0.063, max +0.091). At K = 32 the pooling gather plus `out_projection` over P rows costs at most 0.065 ms (bf16) / 0.027 ms (int4) on top of the plain global pass, and the patch select, raw-id accept and rollback at most 0.010 / 0.015 ms on top of the plain new-patch decoder |
| **H49** end-to-end bf16 | **Supported** | Default prompts, 256 new bytes, two runs per cell (pair gaps <= 0.4%): P4 **1.934 ms/byte** against P0 (fused, K = 8, the best K measured) 2.798 = **+30.9%**; against P0 K = 6 (3.311) +41.6%. Identical-output subset (the 2 prompts whose text equals the minimal loop's in both loops): 2.095 vs 3.376 = **+37.9%**; the 4 prompts on which P0 and P4 emit the same text: 1.892 vs 2.885 = +34.4%. Minimal loop 3.975: P4 is 2.06x faster |
| **H50** cost model | **Supported** | Prediction updated with Stage 0 (cost model with the measured extra, K chosen on the extended set): bf16 2.213, int4 1.458 ms/byte on the default prompts. Measured 1.934 (**-12.6%**) and 1.456 (**-0.1%**). The bf16 miss is the same direction and size as P0's (predicted 3.377, measured 2.798, -17.1%): near-tie divergences change the text of 3 of the 5 prompts, which is more draftable (P4 3.05 bytes/round measured against 2.77 simulated). Extended set: bf16 2.001 vs 1.982 (+1.0%), int4 1.209 vs 1.206 (+0.2%) |
| **H51** exactness | **Supported** | fp32, 30 prompts x {256, 1024} new bytes: **60/60 identical** to the recorded minimal loop in bytes and flags (903-1024 atoms at 1024, limited by the 512-patch static cache as in the earlier tasks). CPU toy test for multi-patch rounds: 26 tests pass, 6 injected bugs each break it |
| **H52** int4 | **Supported** | All-int4, default prompts: P4 **1.456** (k_cross 16, k_max 4) against P0 K = 4 1.686 = **+13.6%** (K = 12 across: 1.455); identical-output subset (4 prompts) +15.1% |
| **H53** byte tax | **Supported** | Same-session block, bf16: P4 1.930 / OLMo 2 combined lookup K = 8 1.419 = **1.361x** (run 1 1.363, run 2 1.359); the limit was 1.7x. The registered prediction near 1.6x was too pessimistic |

Predictions for the record: "the extra crossing cost is under 1 ms in bf16": right, it is 0.04 ms. "P4 lands at 25-33% faster than P0 in bf16": right (30.9% on all prompts, 34-38% on the subsets); "0-10% in int4": **wrong, in the favourable direction** (13.6%; the patch-aligned simulation had +8.9% with a single K, the separate k_max / k_cross gains the rest). "The ratio to drafted OLMo 2 ends near 1.6x": **wrong, 1.36x** (P4 is 1.934 ms/byte against the simulation's 2.259, and OLMo's combined lookup K = 8 is 1.419).

### Deviations and things to know

- **Two Ks.** The patch-aligned simulation used one K for both round types (32). The built policy has `draft.k_max` for rounds that start mid-patch (as in P0) and `draft.k_cross` for crossing rounds; `aligned.simulate_policy` / `plan_round` got an optional `k_within` for it (default None = old behaviour, test added). K was chosen on the 25 extended prompts with the Stage 0 cost: **bf16 k_cross 32, k_max 6; int4 k_cross 16, k_max 4**. The task text names 12 for int4; (12, 4) was also measured end to end and ties (1.455 vs 1.456 ms/byte).
- A boundary round whose lookup draft carries no flagged atom (P = 1) runs the existing single-patch graphs (`encd{K}`, `glob`, `decd{K}p`); the new `gx{P}` / `decx{K}` graphs run for P >= 2 (`gx1` exists for the Stage 0 comparison only). The accept rule is the same raw-id equality in both cases.
- The flags of a crossing draft come from re-tokenizing **the whole context** plus the draft with dolma2 (`cut` protocol, exactly `RecordedSources.lookup_flagged`), every boundary round. Measured: **142 us (bf16) / 123 us (int4) per call**, 0.41-0.43 calls per round; propose + retokenize averages 88 / 73 us per round. The simulation assumed 150 us. Not optimized (no window): the host time is hidden by nothing, the GPU idles during it.
- The static global cache has 512 slots. Crossing drafts are cut to `min(max_patches, free slots)`; a boundary round at a full cache raises. 1024-byte runs of dense-patch prompts stop at 903 atoms (the recorded truth stops there too), as in the earlier tasks.
- The max-patches cap (8) cut a crossing draft in 395 of about 890 boundary rounds on the extended set in bf16 (K = 32 covers about 9.7 patches on average), 22 of about 900 in int4 (K = 16). Raising the cap was not tried.
- The toy test covers the host plan, the shared device pieces (`select_patch_values`, `rollback_index`, `patches_kept`, `accepted_raw`) and the loop with a recurrent toy "global model"; the CUDA graphs themselves are covered by the fp32 run of H51, not by a unit test.
- Stage 0 microbenchmarks use L = 0 (nothing accepted), like the `*-fused-L0` benches the cost model is built on; a real round with larger L commits more positions, which both models include or ignore alike. Negative "extra" values (noise, down to -0.15 ms against the stored inputs) are clamped to 0 in the updated cost model.
- Default path: `draft.policy` defaults to `"within"`; `draft_core.py` and `bolmo_loop.py` are byte-identical to before (hash guards), the gated modules are imported only behind the policy guard, `DraftEngine` got one empty hook (`_extra_fns`). Tests added: `tests/test_draft_gated_toy.py` (26), config validation, import guards, `k_within`. `python -m pytest -q`: 242 passed.

### 1. Hypotheses

Copied verbatim above before any measurement; verdicts in the table above.

### 2. Stage 0: the cost of one crossing round

Built first as graphs on Task A's fused verification path (`byteprof/optim/bolmo_gated.py`): `encd{K}` (unchanged) -> `gx{P}` (gather the encoder outputs at the P flagged inputs, `out_projection`, the 16 global layers over P rows in one pass on the static KV cache at rows pos .. pos+P-1) -> `decx{K}` (the decoder over K + 1 positions with the vector of each position's patch, `lm_head`, accept on raw ids = byte and flag, Task A's lazy commit of the mLSTM states, `glob.pos += patches kept`, `last_value` = the last kept patch). The toy test (`tests/test_draft_gated_toy.py`) was written before the integration. Microbenchmark `tools/p4_cross_bench.py`, results in `results/p4/stage0/` (`report.md`): GPU ms of the three graphs replayed back to back, 120 replays x 5 trials, medians, the compared graphs interleaved trial by trial, extra = X - (pair(K) + B(P) + dp) with pair, B(P) (the global layers over P rows without pooling) and dp (C_p - C_n at K = 0) measured in the same session.

| P | K = 8 | K = 16 | K = 32 |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|

| 4 | 10.085 / +0.056 / +0.064 | 10.053 / +0.041 / -0.097 | 10.307 / +0.010 / +0.056 |
| 6 | 10.091 / -0.003 / +0.031 | 10.090 / +0.069 / -0.098 | 10.339 / +0.010 / +0.049 |

(All P = 1..8 are in `report.md`.) A crossing round with 8 patches costs the same as one with 2 in bf16 within 0.5 ms (B(P) is flat: 7.7 -> 8.3 ms), and in int4 it costs about +1.0 ms (tinygemm leaves its bandwidth regime past M = 4), all of it already in B(P). Pooling and patch select are free at this precision.

| precision | | rounds | bytes/round | bytes/global pass | boundary rounds | patches per boundary round | ms/byte |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
